TikTok: app moved into the organization **ClipTroop**, owner = the personal account; Sandbox updated (name ClipTroop, icon, category Productivity, description, Terms / Privacy / Web on app.cliptroop.com, redirect URIs app. + staging.); the Production version only saves together with a review submission (explanation of each product/scope + demo video), so it waits for the approval videos. The app only uses `user.info.basic` + `video.publish` with FILE_UPLOAD (no "Verify domains" for pull_by_url needed); `video.upload` comes with Content Posting API. Facebook Page connect showed Meta's "App not active" on Oct 7 and again Oct 8 (after 1.9.9). Likely: Meta's crawler got 403 on our public pages (robots.txt block, fixed in 1.9.8 with the facebookexternalhit group), which makes Meta flag the privacy policy / data deletion URLs as invalid and can restrict the app; check App Dashboard for a red "required action" / restriction notice and that the logging-in account has an accepted app role. Earlier note: the app is in Development mode, so only accounts with a role on it (App roles → Roles: Administrator / Developer / Tester, accepted, not pending) can log in; the Facebook account used must also manage the Page. Still to do: 1.9.5's robots fix live → Meta data deletion URL = https://app.cliptroop.com/data-deletion; team members reinstall the app from app.cliptroop.com; check Vercel Function Region matches the Supabase region (Vercel's default is Washington iad1; Frankfurt = fra1), the cheapest speed-up before Supabase Pro.

Since 1.9.2, /setup shows the TARGET addresses (production: NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL or app.cliptroop.com; staging branch: STAGING_URL or staging.cliptroop.com) even when opened on the old vercel.app address, with cards: 1 connect the subdomain (Vercel → Domains + cPanel CNAME), 2 Resend on cliptroop.com (records in cPanel, DMARC `v=DMARC1; p=none;`, new API key, Supabase SMTP: smtp.resend.com 465 user `resend`, sender hello@cliptroop.com / name ClipTroop), 3 Supabase URL Configuration, 4 email templates, 5 platform return addresses, 6 website / privacy / terms / domain verification (Google Search Console + TikTok URL properties via TXT in cPanel), 7 Vercel env (EMAIL_FROM `ClipTroop <alerts@cliptroop.com>`, NEXT_PUBLIC_CONTACT_EMAIL hello@cliptroop.com), 8 (1.9.3) the timer's Vault `posting_url`. GitHub repo renamed to viverrodev/cliptroop on Oct 5 2026 (Vercel kept deploying). Rename the Vercel project LAST, after cards 1–8 on both copies. Resend's free plan allows 3 domains, so viverro.com can stay until the switch is done; then delete it and the old key.
1. Buy the domain + cPanel hosting (same provider, so DNS is in cPanel → Zone Editor).
2. Vercel → Settings → Domains: add `app.<domain>` (Production) and `staging.<domain>` (Preview, Git branch `staging`); copy the CNAME value Vercel shows for each.
3. cPanel → Zone Editor → the domain → Add Record: CNAME `app` → that value, CNAME `staging` → that value. Root records stay. Wait for Vercel's "Valid Configuration".
4. Supabase (each project): Authentication → URL Configuration: Site URL = its new address, add `https://<it>/**` to Redirect URLs (keep the old ones for now); then paste the email templates.
5. Google (Branding: authorized domain, home / privacy / terms; Clients → Web client: JS origins + redirect URIs for both), Search Console domain verification (TXT in cPanel); Meta (App domains, privacy / terms / data deletion URLs, Valid OAuth Redirect URIs, Instagram business login redirect); TikTok (Login Kit redirect URIs in Sandbox and Production, Terms / Privacy / Web URL, URL properties → Domain via TXT). Keep the old addresses until the move is done.
6. Vercel env: Production `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` + `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` = `https://app.<domain>`; Preview `STAGING_URL` = `https://staging.<domain>`. Redeploy production and staging (NEXT_PUBLIC_ is baked in at build).
7. Test: sign in on the new address, invite a test account (email → /welcome → setup), connect YouTube, push test. From now on the old vercel.app addresses move to the new ones by themselves.
8. Everyone: open the new address, sign in once, re-add the app to the home screen from it and turn notifications on again (an installed app belongs to its address; the old one now opens the new address in a browser view).
9. Optional: Resend → add the new domain (DNS records in cPanel), then Supabase SMTP sender + `EMAIL_FROM` on it (viverro.com keeps working meanwhile).

## Rules that keep production safe while we build
- Migrations are additive (expand, then contract in a later release); never drop or rename in the same release that stops using something.
- Unfinished features ship behind a feature flag (off in production) instead of waiting on a branch.
- Every zip: changed files only at their real paths, version bumped, a Version history line. New migrations are NOT pasted by hand any more (since Oct 3 2026): they go in `supabase/migrations/` and the Database Action applies them (push to staging → staging, merge staging into main with a pull request → production). Both projects were baselined through 0064 by hand (0063/0064 were pasted, then `scripts/db/baseline.sql` with 64). Never edit a migration that already ran; changes go in a new numbered file. Tell the owner to check the Database run is green.
- Merging: always a pull request staging → main with "Create a merge commit" (never squash), never delete the staging branch.
- CI must be green on `staging` before merging into `main`; a new migration ships in the same push as the code that needs it (staging gets it first through the Database Action).

## Working notes for Claude
- npm installs: some sandboxes refuse the registry (403 by policy), others allow it (1.16.0 installed pdf-lib and @pdf-lib/fontkit). When it's refused, the owner runs `npm install <pkg>` locally and sends the next zip with node_modules. Either way a new dependency means the owner runs `npm install` after unzipping (package.json and package-lock.json change).
- Preview testing: copy the project, stub next/font (no Google Fonts here), allow /dev-preview in middleware, render the real components with mock data, drive with Playwright (Chromium preinstalled). Start the preview server with dummy `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` (never the real .env.local) and `setsid` so it survives. Never kill processes by matching "next dev" (pkill or a /proc loop: it kills the shell); kill by port with `lsof -t -i :3456`.
- Full migration chain without Supabase: `supabase/tests/supabase-stub.sql` (roles, auth.users + auth.uid/role/jwt, storage buckets/objects + foldername, vault, cron.job/schedule, net.http_post, the realtime publication) + `TEST_DB_URL=postgresql://postgres@/postgres?host=/tmp/vp-pg&port=5499 bash scripts/db/test-from-scratch.sh` (run as the postgres user). For before/after checks keep a database: apply the stub + every migration but the new one, dump `pg_policies`, apply it, diff.
- Browser-feature checks (notifications, service worker, offline): Playwright `chromium.launch({ channel: "chromium" })` (the default headless shell reports Notification.permission "denied"); real push delivery can't be tested here (no push service), the crypto is covered by the RFC test vector. Offline: install the SW with `launchPersistentContext`, stop the server (fuser -k 3456/tcp), relaunch and navigate.
- Domain availability without a registrar: RDAP via WebFetch, `https://pubapi.registry.google/rdap/domain/<name>.app` and `https://rdap.verisign.com/com/v1/domain/<name>.com` (404 = not registered; premium prices aren't visible).
- Visual check without node_modules (1.22.0): `scratchpad/harness/` style: bundle a harness entry that imports the real components with esbuild (`/opt/npm-tools/node_modules/esbuild`, React 19 from `~/.npm-global/lib/node_modules`, stand-ins for next/link, next/navigation, next/dynamic, server-only, the Supabase browser client and the server actions), compile the CSS with Tailwind v4's `compile()` (`@import "tailwindcss"; @config` with a JS copy of tailwind.config.ts, then globals.css without its `@tailwind` lines; candidates = every class-like word in app/components/modules, keeping brackets whole), serve `out/`, mock `fetch` for the routes, and shoot with Playwright (Chromium in /opt/pw-browsers) at 1360 and 390 wide, light and dark, plus hovers and clicks. Tailwind v4 differs a little from v3 (shadows, ring widths): good for layout and behaviour, not for pixel colours.
- Script screens without TipTap (1.23.0): `scratchpad/harness/scripts-harness.tsx` (`ENTRY=scripts-harness.tsx OUTJS=out/scripts.js node build.mjs`, `PAGE=scripts.html node shoot.mjs <scene>`): every `@tiptap/*` import and the scripts' server actions are stand-ins, so the panels, cards, link window, grid and comments render from mock data over static paper HTML (a fake editor `{ view: { dom } }` drives `GrammarLayer`). Scene `paginate` runs the real `measurePages` on a fake document over a real browser layout, applies the breaks and measures again until stable, then checks every page's text starts at its top margin and none sits in a bottom margin (`LOGS=1` prints it). Typed TipTap / ProseMirror stand-ins for type-checking editor code (`scratchpad/tc/pm/pm.d.ts`, `tsconfig.grammar.json`, `tcg.sh`): the main stand-ins type TipTap as `any`, which hides mistakes.
- Pages checks without TipTap (1.23.1): `scratchpad/harness/scripts-harness.tsx` scene `paginate` (`&cols=1|2|3`) builds the document's DOM itself (a contenteditable `.script-doc` inside a paged `.script-paper`), runs the real `measurePages` and applies its shifts as transforms (what the node decorations do), checks every block sits on its page inside the margins, the page after a break, each page's first block at its top margin, and that measuring again gives the same moves; `scratchpad/proto/typing.mjs` types at the end of page 1 in Pages and Grid until the paragraph moves to page 2 and checks the text above stayed, the caret went with it, and Backspace brings it back. `node tw.mjs` rebuilds the harness CSS from app/globals.css (needed after CSS changes). A plain-contenteditable prototype (`scratchpad/proto/index.html`, `run.mjs`) showed Chromium handles transformed blocks: clicks put the caret in the moved block, ArrowDown goes from page 1's end to page 2, drag-selecting across pages works, a click on empty paper does nothing (hence `caretNear`).
- Don't use the Agent tool for side research in this project (the owner declined it once); do it inline.
- Type check with the real Supabase types (1.36.1): the sandbox's `@supabase/*` stand-ins type every query as `any`, so they missed a select built at run time, which the real client types as `GenericStringError` (CI failed on 1.36.0). Clone `supabase/supabase-js` at the locked version (`--branch v2.118.0`; postgrest-js and the rest are under packages/core) and `supabase/ssr` (`--branch v0.5.2`) through the git proxy, and point tsc at their sources with `paths` (`@supabase/supabase-js/dist/module/lib/types` → supabase-js's src/lib/types.ts); leave out the packages' own errors. In code: read a row from a run-time select `as Record<string, unknown>`.
- PDFs in the harness (1.37.0): pdf-lib's own browser build loads fine, but @pdf-lib/fontkit isn't in the sandbox (no registry), so the harness serves a minimal TrueType reader in its place (`stubs/fontkit-lite.js`, no subsets) and its scenes patch `PDFDocument.prototype.embedFont` to `subset: false`. Rasterise the PDF with `pdftoppm` and compare it with the SVG preview; `pdftotext` and `pdfinfo` check the text and the metadata.
- Build each zip's file list against what's on GitHub, not against the last version made here: `git fetch origin staging`, then `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=ACMR FETCH_HEAD HEAD` (and `--diff-filter=D` for files to delete). 1.37.0 was pushed on top of 1.36.0 without the 1.36.1 zip, so CI failed again on 1.36.0's ten errors in `getShortSettings`; the 1.37.0 zip didn't carry that file because it changed in 1.36.1.
- Word files in the harness (1.38.0): the sandbox has docx 9.8.1 (`~/.npm-global/lib/node_modules/docx/dist/index.iife.js`); copy it to the harness's `out/vendor/docx.iife.js` and the app's Word exports run for real (unzip the .docx and read `word/document.xml`). PDF links are inside compressed object streams: `qpdf --qdf --object-streams=disable` first, then look for `/URI`.
- `next build` catches what tsc doesn't (e.g. a Node-only import reachable from the Edge build): build the preview copy before every zip.
- Supabase JWT signing keys: switching the projects to asymmetric keys (Dashboard → Project Settings → JWT Keys → migrate) makes the middleware's `getClaims()` verify locally (no auth round trip per page).
- SQL checks without Supabase: a throwaway Postgres 16 (`initdb` + `pg_ctl` as the postgres user, port 5499, socket in /tmp/vp-pg), a stub of the tables/functions the migration touches (roles anon/authenticated, `auth.uid()` from `request.jwt.claim.sub`), apply the migration twice (`psql -1`), then run each case as a user with `set_config('request.jwt.claim.sub', …)` + `set local role authenticated`.
- 1.9.1 screens were checked with a scratch Playwright script (welcome invite / expired / recovery, hash error → /welcome, 4-step setup on a phone, Staging done before / burst / after / undo confirm with `MOCK_DONE=1`, /setup + email preview, Settings → Account + log out confirm, phone dashboard Customize). Mock note: `verifyOtp` returns no session in the mock, so /welcome's button lands on /login there (real Supabase signs you in).
- Loading screens: start the mock with `MOCK_DELAY=1200`. A Playwright screenshot of a FIRST load waits for the whole stream (the splash is gone by then): check the splash on `/dev-preview/splash` (scratch preview page) and its streaming with a raw http.get that logs chunk times. Client moves screenshot fine. To catch stuck moves, repeat a click 6–10 times and check the URL changes (scratch `t26.cjs`), compare against the previous version built separately.
- Real pages with sample data: `scripts/dev-mock/` (stand-in Supabase on :54321 + fixtures + signed-in Playwright screenshots). Use it for any UI change; extend `fixtures.cjs` when a page reads a new table (`MOCK_LOG=1` lists every query and "no fixture for …").
- Fonts without Google: `NEXT_FONT_GOOGLE_MOCKED_RESPONSES=<file.cjs>` (Next's own test hook): a CommonJS map from the Google Fonts CSS URLs in app/layout.tsx (four in 1.41.0: Fraunces 500;600;700, Fraunces italic, Inter, JetBrains Mono; copy them from the build error) to `/* latin */ @font-face {…; src: url(/absolute/path) format(…)}` blocks. Local files: Inter OTFs in /usr/share/fonts/opentype/inter, a Fraunces 600 woff2 at /mnt/skills/examples/morning/assets/fonts, DejaVuSansMono for JetBrains Mono. Then `next build` and `next dev` work offline with near-real fonts (no next/font stub needed).
- `next dev` can be OOM-killed (the sandbox's ~8 GB) when a sweep compiles every route; a dead server still "answers" in Playwright through the service worker (status 200, ~940 ms each), so read the server log. For sweeps: `next build` with the stand-in env, then `next start -p 3456` against the mock.
- Full-page Playwright screenshots can catch CSS animations half-way (the objectives' rings): pass `animations: "disabled"` to `page.screenshot` (finite animations jump to their end).
- Never `pkill -f` a pattern that also appears in your own shell's command line ("next dev", "node server.cjs"): it kills the shell. Stop servers by port (`fuser -k 3456/tcp`, `fuser -k 54321/tcp`).
- Public GitHub repos can be cloned read-only through the git proxy (add_repo → `git clone --depth 1`); that's how the world map data was fetched.
- No node_modules and no npm registry (Oct 24 2026 sandbox: `registry.npmjs.org` doesn't resolve and the proxy refuses it; CDNs too): the repo itself is public, clone `staging` read-only through the git proxy; React/Node types come from a sparse clone of DefinitelyTyped (`types/react`, `types/react-dom`, `types/node/v22`) plus `frenic/csstype` and `nodejs/undici` (`types/`), stand-ins for next / Supabase / TipTap etc. in a scratch `node_modules` (gitignored) for `tsc` and the tests that import them. A hand edit of package-lock.json is checked with `npm ci --dry-run --offline` (it builds the ideal tree from the lock; anything inconsistent needs a fetch and fails with ENOTCACHED) and Arborist's `loadVirtual()` (no invalid edges). npm audit can't run here: drive `scripts/audit.mjs` with a fake `npm` on PATH.
- Dashboard widgets are loaded on demand (1.15.0): a new widget = its `dynamic()` line + `PRELOAD` entry in `modules/dashboard/components/studio.tsx`. To see a widget's failure box, abort its chunk in Playwright (`page.route("**/_next/static/chunks/<file>", (r) => r.abort())`; find the file by grepping `.next/static/chunks` for a text the widget shows).
- Mock (1.19.0): `scripts/dev-mock/live.cjs` (always on): ~56 hours of counts around the real "now" for the busiest videos (every 10 min) and the next 30 (every 3 h), and `analytics_live_hours` / `analytics_live_gains` worked out like the database; `MOCK_LIVE=0` (no counts yet), `MOCK_LIVE_FRESH=1` (realtime started 3 hours ago), `MOCK_NO_REACH=1` (no impressions / CTR, the Reporting API note). Live job itself can't run in the mock (no platform tokens): its decisions are covered by tests/live-math.test.ts (`snapshotsOf`, `gainedBetween`, `perHour`, `liveDue`, `hourName`) and tests/reach.test.ts; the SQL by supabase/tests/live-numbers.test.sql; and the whole thing end to end by `scripts/e2e/run.sh` (a throwaway database with every migration, PostgREST v12.2.3 in front like Supabase, the real lib/live.ts and sync.ts with fake platform APIs: full pass finds all 230 videos, hot calls ~3 Data API units, teammates-only sums, reach reports → impressions / CTR, a TikTok pass resumed from its cursor, Instagram, Facebook, LIVE_MINUTES, the Reporting-API-off note, the TikTok morning copy's per-video day). `TEST_DB_URL=… [POSTGREST_BIN=…] scripts/e2e/run.sh`; all green on Oct 14 2026.
- Mock (1.18.0): `MOCK_LAYOUT=streak` puts the Win streak widget first (`MOCK_STREAK_SIZE=4x3`, default 3x3); `MOCK_NO_DAILY=1` pauses "Two shorts a day", so the streak counts weeks (with `MOCK_CHEER=1` the dashboard shows the away cheers and then the streak card). Loading screens: `MOCK_DELAY=900`, then click a task's link and screenshot at once.
- Mock (1.17.0): filters `like` / `ilike` (PostgREST patterns) and sorts by `order` (the row's own columns); `job_runs` has four sample runs; the Advanced videos have day-by-day views since they were posted (YouTube per day, the others' running totals for 12 days). Video pages: `/analytics/video/youtube/ytS006xyz`, `/analytics/video/instagram/inR005`, `/analytics/video/youtube/ytL002xyz`.
- Advanced (1.16.0) in the mock: always on (`scripts/dev-mock/advanced.cjs`). Screens checked with a scratch Playwright script (each platform, Shorts / long, phone, dark, the video drawer, the export dialog, the chart's hover); Download for Claude end to end with `page.route` stubs for `**/api/analytics/thumb**` and the `video-frames` images (the mock serves neither), then the PDF opened page by page (pdftoppm) and the text read. pdf-lib fonts: subsetting Inter dropped letters (so `subset: false`), embedding Fraunces whole broke its spacing (so `subset: true`); check both after touching `build-pdf.ts`. Frame capture checked in Chromium with generated .webm files (H.264 doesn't play there).
- Mock flags added in 1.15.0: `MOCK_PROJECTIONS=1` (with MOCK_OBJECTIVES=1: eight projections with daily points: running, reached, ended, money), `MOCK_SWITCHED=1` (the Facebook Page replaced 5 days ago: Analytics' account note), `MOCK_COMMENTS=1` (comments on short #231's script, two on rewritten words).
- Outliers (1.27.0) checks: `npx tsx scripts/dev-mock/make-outliers.ts` (sample, then restart the mock), `node scripts/dev-mock/outliers-shot.cjs <dir> "" --size=phone|laptop|wide [--dark] [--view]` (with `SHOT_FONTS=<folder with @fontsource-variable/fraunces + inter>` when Google Fonts are blocked), cut tall phone shots into side-by-side strips to read them. The e2e (`scripts/e2e/run.sh`) needs `PGTZ=UTC` when the local Postgres isn't in UTC (live.e2e compares a timestamp's text). `buildInsights` takes ~2.7 s for 30,000 videos (both kinds; 90 KB of insights), `generateIdeas` ~15 ms for 24 ideas. `/outliers` first load: 166 kB (the other tabs are their own chunks).
- Server actions and ProseMirror (1.27.5): never pass `editor.getJSON()` (or anything with null-prototype objects) to a server action as it is: React serializes such objects as temporary references and the server gets `"$T"` / nothing. Send `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(x))` (`readyToSave` does). Symptom: saved documents with `{"type":"image"}` and no `attrs`; check with the mock's `MOCK_WRITES=scripts`.
- Glass (1.27.5): `backdrop-filter` (like `transform` / `filter`) makes its element the containing block of `position: fixed` children, so a pop-up drawn inside a blurred card would be trapped in it. Blur only the shell bars, dashboard widgets (they're transformed anyway) and `.glass-pane` pop-ups that are portaled to `<body>`; ordinary cards only get see-through backgrounds. With the glass look the page background is on `<html>` and `<body>` stays transparent (a painted body would cover anything at a negative z-index, like the dashboard background).
- Mock flags added in 1.27.5: `MOCK_SCRIPT=<doc.json>`, `MOCK_COMMENTS=many`, `MOCK_MEETING=soon`, `MOCK_STRANGER=1` (/u/radu), `MOCK_GLASS=1`, `MOCK_DASH_BG=jpg|gif|webm|mp4` with `MOCK_DASH_BG_FILE` / `MOCK_DASH_BG_DIM`; storage uploads, copies and removals are accepted (`MOCK_UPLOAD_FAIL=1` refuses). Firefox printing can't be tested here (only Chromium is installed): the owner's blank-page PDF came from Firefox (its PDF Producer says so); the fix was checked with Chromium's PDF output of the same script (8 pages, none blank, no comment marks), so the owner should try it once in Firefox.
- The zip includes .env.local (production secrets) and local HTTPS certificates: never publish them; production keys get rotated at the production cut-over anyway.

- Glass in screenshots (1.28.0): headless Chromium's default software compositor draws `backdrop-filter` wrong (the sharp content shows through a faint blur), which looks like "the blur doesn't work". Launch with `args: ["--use-angle=swiftshader", "--enable-unsafe-swiftshader", "--ignore-gpu-blocklist"]` to get the GPU path; then it renders like a real browser.
- Glass everywhere (1.28.0): with "Everywhere" every positioned `bg-surface` pop-up that has a shadow gets a `backdrop-filter`, so it becomes the containing block of any `position: fixed` element inside it. Pop-ups that open their own fixed menus (select lists, date pickers, anchored menus) must keep portaling them to `<body>`, as they do now.
- Boards (1.28.0): `GridBoard` is shared by the dashboard and profiles; change drag / resize there once. Its top resize strip is 14 px tall, so a test that grabs a card to move it should press on the title (about 24 px down), not the very top.
- Mock flags added in 1.28.0: `MOCK_GLASS_STYLE=liquid`, `MOCK_GLASS_SCOPE=everywhere`, `MOCK_GLASS_SIDEBAR=1`, `MOCK_GLASS_LEVEL=0..100`; `MOCK_WALLPAPERS=1..3` and `MOCK_WALL_LUMA=0..100|null` (with `MOCK_DASH_BG`); `MOCK_PROFILE_FRAME=<frame>`, `MOCK_PROFILE_BG=1`, `MOCK_PRIVATE=maria|radu`; `profile_stats` and `get_visible_teams` answer from fixtures; `MOCK_WRITES` tables also remember deletes, and inserts get ids.
- React 19 + Next server actions (1.41.0): `useDeferredValue` (and any transition) is drawn together with every transition in flight, and each server action call is one (Next's `callServer` runs inside `startTransition`), so a deferred value waits for any save or load to come back (with MOCK_DELAY: seconds). For a view that must keep up while actions run (the media kit's preview), use an ordinary state update after the next paint (`useLaidOut` in media-kit-tab.tsx). To see it: a MutationObserver on the pages' `aria-busy` while a save is pending.
- QA (1.41.0): the "is here" What's new card's mascot (SVG animations) stalls frames under swiftshader (0 fps after a toast), so page screenshots can take 30–50 s or time out: mark it seen in the context (`localStorage` `vp-whats-new-seen` and `vp-whats-new-told` = package.json's version) unless the card is what's tested. Playwright won't click an `aria-disabled` button (the Select bar's one-at-a-time Delete): `click({ force: true })`. The mock's images from http://127.0.0.1:54321 are refused by the CSP (https only): ignore those console errors; they can also make `page.addStyleTag` throw (catch it). The kit's pages on a phone are behind Set up / Preview: hidden pages don't run their animations (check them with the Preview on).
- Mock flags added in 1.41.0: `MOCK_LIBRARY=1` (about 1,500 more videos, every platform, since 2021), `MOCK_KIT=owner|eight`, `MOCK_KIT_TEMPLATES=N`, `MOCK_DELETE=maria:shorts,andrei:both`, `MOCK_DELETE_MANY=shorts,longs`, `MOCK_NO_0119=1` (with `MOCK_WRITES=short_videos` a master's old-way delete answers its rows); slow thumbnails in Playwright: delay the `**/api/analytics/thumb**` route.
- Mock flags added in 1.42.0: `MOCK_BUSINESS=1` (Business on for the team with a year and a half of books, three assets, an inventory, a closed 2025, history) or `fresh` (on, nothing set up), `MOCK_BUSINESS_ACCESS=maria:view,andrei:edit` (default maria:view), `MOCK_NO_0120=1` (as before 0120); BNR's rates at `/bnr/` (start the app with `BNR_RATES_URL=http://127.0.0.1:54321/bnr/`; Vercel ignores it, staging and production). With writes on: `MOCK_WRITES=business_entries,business_profiles,business_sources,business_assets,business_inventories,business_inventory_items,business_ref_lines,business_files,business_access,team_features,business_years`.
- QA (1.42.0): the production CSP refuses the mock's plain-http address from the browser, so anything the page sends Supabase itself (a storage upload, `my_progress`) fails in a `next start` run: open the Playwright context with `bypassCSP: true` for those (the CSP lines in the console are that, not a bug). Downloads with Romanian letters in their name come out as `download` unless the browser runs with a UTF-8 locale (`LANG=C.UTF-8 LC_ALL=C.UTF-8`; the sandbox has none): the app's names are right.
- Business's two languages (1.42.0): each file keeps its words as `const W = words({ key: { en, ro } })` (`modules/business/lib/i18n.ts`; a sentence with values is the same function in both) and reads them with `const w = useWords(W)` (`modules/business/components/context.tsx`; the page's `lang` is `b.lang`); counts through `nEn` / `nRo` (Romanian puts "de" from 20 on, not after 101–119). The server's messages are `MSG` in `modules/business/lib/messages.ts`, picked by `say()` from the cookie in every action. Numbers and days: `formatLei(x, b.lang)`, `formatDay(d, b.lang)` (`parseAmount` reads both ways). A new word on the page goes in its file's `W` with both languages: `tests/business-words.test.ts` fails on English written straight into the markup (text, labels, aria-labels, placeholders, options, confirm windows, toasts), on a pair missing a language or a value, and on English in a Romanian sentence. The shared controls' words are `uiWords` in `components/ui/ui-locale.tsx` (English unchanged; `<UiLocale value="ro">` turns them Romanian). QA: a Playwright context with the cookie `vp_business_lang=ro` added before the first page; scan the page's `[lang="ro"]` root, its windows, menus, lists and toasts for English words (Romanian also has "a", "an", "are", "in", "fiscal", "export", "total", "document", "net", "rate", "note", "plan", "card").
- Notes only for some teams (1.42.0): a feature turned on team by team (Developer → Features, `team_features`) gets `only: "business"` on its release (or on a change) in `lib/changelog.ts`; What's new shows it only where the person has it (`whatsNewFacts(v, { business })`, `forAudience`), the site never (`PUBLIC_CHANGELOG`). A new gated feature: add its name to `Gated` in `lib/changelog-meta.ts`, the `team_features.feature` check (a new migration) and the layout's audience.

## What to upload in a new conversation
This handoff (paste it first) + the latest full project zip (with node_modules). Since 1.14.0 the handoff is also saved in the claude.ai Project "VPlanner" (claude/VPLANNER_HANDOFF.md), and the repo is public (eduardneague/cliptroop, branch `staging` = the newest code), so a conversation in that Project can start from those two when no zip is attached.
